Insurance companies, I swear, they just want to collect your premiums every month and then fight you tooth and nail when you actually need a payout. It's a racket. \n\nIt took four months to get that basement put back together. We ended up putting in that luxury vinyl plank flooring instead of carpet the second time around. It's much better for moisture, obviously, and it actually looks just like real hardwood. You wouldn't even know the difference. My wife picked out this really nice distressed hickory color that completely brightened up the room... \n\nBut, uh, I'm sorry, what was the question? Something about the basement? Oh, right, the floating feeling. No, no. Even with all the stress of the flood and arguing with Greg, I was completely grounded. Never felt anything like this before.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these unreal or floating feelings happen more often when you're reminded of the client who threatened you?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... yeah, maybe a little bit. It's weird. Like, what brings me in is that I keep having these moments where my own house or office feels unreal. Like I'm watching myself from across the room instead of actually being there. It definitely happens when I think about the guy, but sometimes it just happens randomly, too. \n\nLike the other day, I was sitting in my living room, looking at the coffee table. We just got a new coffee table, actually. Solid oak. My wife found it at this antique store out in the country, about an hour drive from here. The guy who runs the place, his name is Arthur, he's got this incredible collection of mid-century modern stuff. But the prices! You'd think he was selling gold bricks instead of furniture. We haggled with him for a good forty-five minutes. I told him, 'Arthur, there's a scratch on the left leg, I'm not paying full price.' He tried to tell me it was 'patina' and added to the character of the piece. Patina! It's a scratch from someone dragging a box across it. I know the difference between natural aging and careless handling. \n\nAnyway, we finally got it home, and I was just sitting there looking at it, and suddenly everything felt... fake. Like I wasn't really in the room. I was just hovering somewhere near the ceiling, watching myself look at the coffee table. It's the strangest sensation. \n\nWait, did you ask about the client? Yeah, so, I mean, it happens then, too. But the coffee table thing was definitely real. Or... unreal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the unreal feelings happen more often when you're in your office where the client threatened you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. The office is the worst for it. Like I was saying before, what brings me in is that I keep having these moments where my own house or office feels unreal. Like I'm watching myself from across the room instead of actually being there. It happened just Tuesday when I was sitting at my desk. \n\nI was trying to review a client's Schedule C. The guy runs a small landscaping business, and his bookkeeping is a total disaster. He brings me his receipts in a literal shoebox. A Nike box! I just felt like I wasn't at my desk anymore. I was looking at myself looking at the receipt. The whole office just felt like a movie set or something. So, yeah, it definitely happens a lot in the office.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the unreal feelings happen more often when you see or think about things that remind you of the client who threatened you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Like, even just seeing a certain type of folder sets it off. The guy had this thick, overstuffed manila envelope when he came in—you know the ones with the little red string you wrap around the cardboard circles? I usually buy those in bulk from Staples. Well, actually, I switched to Uline last year because Staples completely jacked up their free shipping minimums for business accounts. Uline is pretty good, though they send you this massive catalog every single month. It's literally the size of a phone book. \n\nDo they even make phone books anymore? I remember when they used to just chuck them onto your driveway in those clear plastic bags. My dad used to use two of the thick yellow pages just to prop up his computer monitor. He had one of those old beige CRT monitors that took up half the desk and weighed like forty pounds. You had to degauss it every once in a while, remember that? It made that loud *thunk* sound...\n\nWait, I'm sorry, I lost my train of thought. Reminders of the client, right. Yeah, if I see one of those string envelopes, or if I just start thinking about what he said to me, it triggers it. Like I've been saying, that's the main thing bringing me in here—I just keep having these weird moments where my own office, or even my house, just feels completely unreal. Like I'm hovering and watching myself from across the room instead of actually being there in my own body. It's incredibly disturbing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think seeing or thinking about the client who threatened you is what usually triggers these unreal feelings?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. It's definitely the main trigger. Like I said, it's the whole reason I'm here... what brings me in is that I keep having these moments where my own house or office feels unreal. Like I'm watching myself from across the room instead of actually being there. And it almost always happens after I've been thinking about him. \n\nLike yesterday, I was just trying to make a sandwich. A turkey club. You know, it's getting really hard to find decent deli meat lately. The supermarket down the street used to carry this incredible peppered turkey breast, sliced thin, just the way I like it. But they switched suppliers a few months ago, and now everything tastes like rubber. And the prices! I was looking at the roast beef the other day, and it was twenty-two dollars a pound. Twenty-two dollars! I mean, I know inflation is hitting everything, I see it in my clients' books all the time. The cost of goods sold is through the roof for everyone. But twenty-two dollars for deli meat? You might as well go out to a steakhouse. \n\nI ended up just getting some pre-packaged ham, which is full of sodium, I know, my doctor is always on me about my blood pressure. But anyway, I was making the sandwich, and I just happened to think about the guy—the client—and boom. Suddenly I'm not in my kitchen anymore. I'm floating up near the cabinets, watching myself put mayo on the bread. It's just... it's really messing with my head.\n\nDoctor: Are these unreal feelings affecting your ability to do your job as a tax preparer?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. I've actually missed several client deadlines recently, which is... I mean, in my line of work, missing a deadline is a cardinal sin. But I just can't seem to focus. And speaking of deadlines, the whole e-file system has been so glitchy this year. Sometimes you hit submit on a return, and it just hangs there on this little spinning loading screen. Then you don't know if it actually went through to the IRS, so you have to go pull the acknowledgment report, which usually means printing it out for the physical file. \n\nAnd don't even get me started on printer ink. Have you bought toner lately? It's like buying liquid gold. I have a Brother laser printer—great machine, really reliable, almost never jams—but the name-brand toner cartridges are eighty bucks a pop now. I tried buying the cheap generic ones off Amazon a few months ago to save some money, but they left these horrible black streaks all down the left side of the page. You can't hand a client their 1040 with black streaks all over it, it looks completely unprofessional. I had to spend an hour taking the whole thing apart and cleaning the drum unit with a Q-tip and rubbing alcohol. You have to be really gentle when you do that, though, or you'll scratch the photosensitive roller, and then the whole unit is garbage... \n\nWait, I'm sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my ability to work. Yeah. So because of these episodes, I've actually completely stopped seeing people at the office. I just don't trust myself to handle their tax returns accurately when my brain is doing this, and honestly, I don't feel like I can keep the workplace safe right now. So I'm just... I'm really falling behind.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any difficulties in your personal relationships due to these unreal feelings?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if it's affecting my relationships, really. I mean, I live alone, so it's not like I have a wife or kids around to notice me spacing out. My ex-wife took the dog when we split up, which was probably for the best because he was a Golden Retriever mix and he shed constantly. You'd vacuum the rug, and ten minutes later there'd be tumbleweeds of dog hair drifting across the hardwood. I bought one of those robot vacuums to try and keep up with it, you know, the ones that just bounce around the room? But it kept getting stuck under the edge of the sofa. It would just sit there beeping sadly until I came and rescued it. Plus, you have to empty the little dustbin on those things every single day, and if you forget, it just smears dirt around... \n\nBut anyway, relationships. Twenty-two dollars! I mean, I know inflation is hitting everything, I see it in my clients' books all the time. The cost of goods sold is through the roof for everyone. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really, no. I mean, outside of work, my routine is pretty simple anyway, so I haven't noticed these feelings getting in the way of normal stuff like laundry or whatever. 

Speaking of laundry, though, my dryer has been making this horrendous squeaking noise lately. It's a gas dryer, which I've always preferred because it heats up so much faster than the electric ones. But this squeak... it sounds like a belt is going bad, or maybe the drum rollers are shot. I actually watched a YouTube video the other night on how to fix it. The guy in the video made it look incredibly easy—just pop two screws out from under the lint trap and the whole front panel comes off. But of course, my model is different. And the replacement parts! A tiny little rubber wheel is like thirty bucks. My parents had this old Maytag when I was growing up that literally lasted twenty-five years without a single repair... 

Um, but anyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, daily activities. Yeah, no, I'm getting my chores and stuff done okay. It's mostly just the work stuff that's really falling apart.

I've actually missed several client deadlines recently, which is... I mean, in my line of work, missing a deadline is a cardinal sin. But I just can't seem to focus. Then you don't know if it actually went through to the IRS, so you have to go pull the acknowledgment report, which usually means printing it out for the physical file. \n\nAnd don't even get me started on printer ink. Have you bought toner lately? It's like buying liquid gold. You can't hand a client their 1040 with black streaks all over it, it looks completely unprofessional. I had to spend an hour taking the whole thing apart and cleaning the drum unit with a Q-tip and rubbing alcohol. You have to be really gentle when you do that, though, or you'll scratch the photosensitive roller, and then the whole unit is garbage... \n\nWait, I'm sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my ability to work. Yeah. So because of these episodes, I've actually completely stopped seeing people at the office. I just don't trust myself to handle their tax returns accurately when my brain is doing this, and honestly, I don't feel like I can keep the workplace safe right now. So I'm just... I'm really falling behind.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any difficulties in your personal relationships due to these unreal feelings?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if it's affecting my relationships, really. I mean, I live alone, so it's not like I have a wife or kids around to notice me spacing out. My ex-wife took the dog when we split up, which was probably for the best because he was a Golden Retriever mix and he shed constantly. You'd vacuum the rug, and ten minutes later there'd be tumbleweeds of dog hair drifting across the hardwood. I bought one of those robot vacuums to try and keep up with it, you know, the ones that just bounce around the room? But it kept getting stuck under the edge of the sofa. I'm mostly just keeping to myself right now anyway, trying to figure out what's going on with my head.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble with daily activities outside of work due to these unreal feelings?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. It's a gas dryer, which I've always preferred because it heats up so much faster than the electric ones. But this squeak... it sounds like a belt is going bad, or maybe the drum rollers are shot. I actually watched a YouTube video the other night on how to fix it. The guy in the video made it look incredibly easy—just pop two screws out from under the lint trap and the whole front panel comes off. But of course, my model is different. You have to wedge a putty knife under the top panel to release these little spring clips, and I ended up scratching the white enamel paint trying to do it. Which is so frustrating. \n\nAnd the replacement parts! A tiny little rubber wheel is like thirty bucks. I don't really know. I mean, I guess it could be the anxiety about that guy. But physical sensations? I haven't really thought about it like that. It's just this weird feeling, you know? 

Speaking of physical sensations, though, I did tweak my lower back the other day. I was trying to move this heavy filing cabinet in my home office. It's one of those old metal ones, four drawers, probably weighs a hundred pounds empty. I should have emptied it first, but I was in a rush because I was trying to make room for this new ergonomic chair I bought online. It’s supposed to be amazing for your posture, has all these adjustable lumbar supports and armrests that move in like six different directions. But the box it came in was massive, and it sat in my hallway for three days because I couldn't figure out how to put the wheels on. The instructions were completely useless—just these tiny diagrams with no words, like reading hieroglyphics. I swear, companies do that on purpose to save money on printing translations. 

Anyway, I finally got it together, but now my back has this dull ache right at the base of my spine. I’ve been using a heating pad, which helps a little, but it has this auto-shutoff feature after twenty minutes, which is so annoying when you just want to sit there and let the heat soak in...

Um, what were we talking about? The unreal feelings? Yeah, I'm not really sure what's causing them, honestly. Whether it's the anxiety or something else, I just don't know.

Um... no, not really. I mean, I still go to work, I still go to the grocery store. Though, speaking of the grocery store, I was at Kroger the other day, the one over on Elm Avenue. They completely rearranged the aisles. It's infuriating! I spent twenty minutes just trying to find the canned tomatoes. Why do they do that? You finally learn where everything is, and then some corporate manager decides the pasta needs to be next to the baking supplies instead of the sauces. It makes no sense. I was talking to the checkout girl about it—she's a sweet kid, maybe nineteen, always wears these oversized glasses. She said they do it so you have to walk past more items and hopefully buy things you didn't come in for. Which, I suppose, is a smart business strategy, but it's incredibly frustrating when you're just trying to make a quick chili. I make a really good chili, by the way. Secret is a dash of cinnamon. It's really just been going on for about two weeks now, ever since that client threatened me in my office. I don't write the IRS guidelines. \n\nSpeaking of the IRS, they changed the formatting on the 1040 again this year. It is a complete nightmare for our software. We use this program, TaxPro Elite, and every time there's a mid-season update, it crashes the entire office network. Last Tuesday, I spent three hours on hold with their tech support. Three hours! And the hold music is this awful, repetitive smooth jazz track. Just a synthesizer and a saxophone playing the same four notes on a loop. I swear they do it on purpose. It's a psychological tactic to make you hang up so they don't have to deal with you. I finally got a hold of a guy named Brent, who sounded like he was eating a sandwich the whole time he was talking to me. You could hear him chewing! No, not really. I mean, I don't try to block it out or anything. If someone asks, I'll tell them. I was actually just on the phone with my brother, Dave, the other night, and we were talking for like an hour. Well, mostly he was talking. \n\nHe just bought a new truck. A Ford F-150. And let me tell you, the interest rates right now are absolutely criminal. As a tax guy, I look at the financing terms he agreed to, and it just makes my stomach turn. I told him, 'Dave, you're paying almost eight percent on a depreciating asset!' But he wouldn't listen. He just had to have the heated seats and the towing package. He doesn't even own a boat! What is he towing? The dealership saw him coming a mile away. They always do that trick where they focus on the monthly payment instead of the total cost of the vehicle. They extend the loan term out to 72 or 84 months so the monthly number looks smaller, but you end up paying thousands more in interest over the life of the loan. I tried to explain the amortization schedule to him, but he just glazed over. People never want to look at the math until tax season rolls around and they wonder where all their money went. \n\nBut... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, avoiding talking about the client. No, no. I don't avoid it. I just don't really try to hide from it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience anything similar to these unreal or floating feelings during any other stressful or traumatic event in your life before this incident with the client?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't. I mean, I've definitely had stressful things happen in my life, but I never felt that weird disconnected, floating thing before. Like, back in 2018, my water heater burst while I was at the office during the peak of tax season. Flooded my entire finished basement. Talk about stressful. The drywall was ruined, the carpet was soaking wet... we had to rip everything right down to the studs. \n\nAnd dealing with the insurance adjuster was an absolute nightmare. Guy's name was Greg, wore these awful yellow suspenders. He kept trying to claim it was a \"negligent maintenance\" issue and therefore wasn't fully covered under our specific policy tier. It's a racket. \n\nIt took four months to get that basement put back together. We ended up putting in that luxury vinyl plank flooring instead of carpet the second time around. My wife picked out this really nice distressed hickory color that completely brightened up the room... \n\nBut, uh, I'm sorry, what was the question? Something about the basement? Oh, right, the floating feeling. No, no. Even with all the stress of the flood and arguing with Greg, I was completely grounded. Never felt anything like this before.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these unreal or floating feelings happen more often when you're reminded of the client who threatened you?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... yeah, maybe a little bit. It's weird. Like, what brings me in is that I keep having these moments where my own house or office feels unreal. Like I'm watching myself from across the room instead of actually being there. It definitely happens when I think about the guy, but sometimes it just happens randomly, too. \n\nLike the other day, I was sitting in my living room, looking at the coffee table. I know the difference between natural aging and careless handling. \n\nAnyway, we finally got it home, and I was just sitting there looking at it, and suddenly everything felt... fake. Yeah, so, I mean, it happens then, too. But the coffee table thing was definitely real. Or... unreal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the unreal feelings happen more often when you're in your office where the client threatened you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Like I'm watching myself from across the room instead of actually being there. It happened just Tuesday when I was sitting at my desk. \n\nI was trying to review a client's Schedule C. I'm floating up near the cabinets, watching myself put mayo on the bread. We haggled with him for a good forty-five minutes. I told him, 'Arthur, there's a scratch on the left leg, I'm not paying full price.' He tried to tell me it was 'patina' and added to the character of the piece. Patina! It's a scratch from someone dragging a box across it. I know the difference between natural aging and careless handling. \n\nAnyway, we finally got it home, and I was just sitting there looking at it, and suddenly everything felt... fake. Like I wasn't really in the room. I was just hovering somewhere near the ceiling, watching myself look at the coffee table. It's the strangest sensation. \n\nWait, did you ask about the client? Yeah, so, I mean, it happens then, too. But the coffee table thing was definitely real. Or... unreal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the unreal feelings happen more often when you're in your office where the client threatened you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. The office is the worst for it. Like I was saying before, what brings me in is that I keep having these moments where my own house or office feels unreal. Like I'm watching myself from across the room instead of actually being there. It happened just Tuesday when I was sitting at my desk. \n\nI was trying to review a client's Schedule C. The guy runs a small landscaping business, and his bookkeeping is a total disaster. He brings me his receipts in a literal shoebox. A Nike box! I've told him a hundred times, \"Frank, you need to use QuickBooks, or at least a spreadsheet.\" But no, every April, it's the shoebox. And half the receipts are faded because he leaves them on the dashboard of his truck in the sun. Thermal paper doesn't survive that! I spend hours just trying to decipher if a receipt is from Home Depot or a gas station. And then he wants to deduct his dog's food as a security expense because the dog rides in the truck with him. I had to explain to him the IRS's definition of an \"ordinary and necessary\" business expense. They don't mess around with that stuff. If you get audited and you're trying to write off Purina Dog Chow for a golden retriever, they're going to throw the book at you. \n\nAnyway, I was staring at this faded receipt, and suddenly... I just felt like I wasn't at my desk anymore. I was looking at myself looking at the receipt. The whole office just felt like a movie set or something. So, yeah, it definitely happens a lot in the office.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the unreal feelings happen more often when you see or think about things that remind you of the client who threatened you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Like, even just seeing a certain type of folder sets it off. The guy had this thick, overstuffed manila envelope when he came in—you know the ones with the little red string you wrap around the cardboard circles? I usually buy those in bulk from Staples. Well, actually, I switched to Uline last year because Staples completely jacked up their free shipping minimums for business accounts. Uline is pretty good, though they send you this massive catalog every single month. It's literally the size of a phone book. \n\nDo they even make phone books anymore? I remember when they used to just chuck them onto your driveway in those clear plastic bags. My dad used to use two of the thick yellow pages just to prop up his computer monitor. He had one of those old beige CRT monitors that took up half the desk and weighed like forty pounds. You had to degauss it every once in a while, remember that? It made that loud *thunk* sound...\n\nWait, I'm sorry, I lost my train of thought. Reminders of the client, right. Yeah, if I see one of those string envelopes, or if I just start thinking about what he said to me, it triggers it. Like I've been saying, that's the main thing bringing me in here—I just keep having these weird moments where my own office, or even my house, just feels completely unreal. Like I'm hovering and watching myself from across the room instead of actually being there in my own body. It's incredibly disturbing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think seeing or thinking about the client who threatened you is what usually triggers these unreal feelings?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. It's definitely the main trigger. Like I said, it's the whole reason I'm here... what brings me in is that I keep having these moments where my own house or office feels unreal. Like I'm watching myself from across the room instead of actually being there. And it almost always happens after I've been thinking about him. \n\nLike yesterday, I was just trying to make a sandwich. A turkey club. You know, it's getting really hard to find decent deli meat lately. The supermarket down the street used to carry this incredible peppered turkey breast, sliced thin, just the way I like it. But they switched suppliers a few months ago, and now everything tastes like rubber. And the prices! I was looking at the roast beef the other day, and it was twenty-two dollars a pound. Twenty-two dollars! I mean, I know inflation is hitting everything, I see it in my clients' books all the time. The cost of goods sold is through the roof for everyone. But twenty-two dollars for deli meat? You might as well go out to a steakhouse. \n\nI ended up just getting some pre-packaged ham, which is full of sodium, I know, my doctor is always on me about my blood pressure. But anyway, I was making the sandwich, and I just happened to think about the guy—the client—and boom. Suddenly I'm not in my kitchen anymore. I'm floating up near the cabinets, watching myself put mayo on the bread. It's just... it's really messing with my head.\n\nDoctor: Are these unreal feelings affecting your ability to do your job as a tax preparer?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. I've actually missed several client deadlines recently, which is... I mean, in my line of work, missing a deadline is a cardinal sin. But I just can't seem to focus. And speaking of deadlines, the whole e-file system has been so glitchy this year. Sometimes you hit submit on a return, and it just hangs there on this little spinning loading screen. Then you don't know if it actually went through to the IRS, so you have to go pull the acknowledgment report, which usually means printing it out for the physical file. \n\nAnd don't even get me started on printer ink. Have you bought toner lately? It's like buying liquid gold. I have a Brother laser printer—great machine, really reliable, almost never jams—but the name-brand toner cartridges are eighty bucks a pop now. I tried buying the cheap generic ones off Amazon a few months ago to save some money, but they left these horrible black streaks all down the left side of the page. You can't hand a client their 1040 with black streaks all over it, it looks completely unprofessional. I had to spend an hour taking the whole thing apart and cleaning the drum unit with a Q-tip and rubbing alcohol. You have to be really gentle when you do that, though, or you'll scratch the photosensitive roller, and then the whole unit is garbage... \n\nWait, I'm sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my ability to work. Yeah. So because of these episodes, I've actually completely stopped seeing people at the office. I just don't trust myself to handle their tax returns accurately when my brain is doing this, and honestly, I don't feel like I can keep the workplace safe right now. So I'm just... I'm really falling behind.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any difficulties in your personal relationships due to these unreal feelings?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if it's affecting my relationships, really. I mean, I live alone, so it's not like I have a wife or kids around to notice me spacing out. My ex-wife took the dog when we split up, which was probably for the best because he was a Golden Retriever mix and he shed constantly. You'd vacuum the rug, and ten minutes later there'd be tumbleweeds of dog hair drifting across the hardwood. I bought one of those robot vacuums to try and keep up with it, you know, the ones that just bounce around the room? But it kept getting stuck under the edge of the sofa. It would just sit there beeping sadly until I came and rescued it. Plus, you have to empty the little dustbin on those things every single day, and if you forget, it just smears dirt around... \n\nBut anyway, relationships. No, I haven't really noticed any issues there. I'm mostly just keeping to myself right now anyway, trying to figure out what's going on with my head.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble with daily activities outside of work due to these unreal feelings?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, outside of work, my routine is pretty simple anyway, so I haven't noticed these feelings getting in the way of normal stuff like laundry or whatever. \n\nSpeaking of laundry, though, my dryer has been making this horrendous squeaking noise lately. It's a gas dryer, which I've always preferred because it heats up so much faster than the electric ones. But this squeak... it sounds like a belt is going bad, or maybe the drum rollers are shot. I actually watched a YouTube video the other night on how to fix it. The guy in the video made it look incredibly easy—just pop two screws out from under the lint trap and the whole front panel comes off. But of course, my model is different. You have to wedge a putty knife under the top panel to release these little spring clips, and I ended up scratching the white enamel paint trying to do it. Which is so frustrating. \n\nAnd the replacement parts! A tiny little rubber wheel is like thirty bucks. It's almost to the point where it's cheaper to just buy a whole new machine, but they just don't make them like they used to, you know? My parents had this old Maytag when I was growing up that literally lasted twenty-five years without a single repair... \n\nUm, but anyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, daily activities. Yeah, no, I'm getting my chores and stuff done okay. It's mostly just the work stuff that's really falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping since these unreal feelings started?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I wouldn't say I've had any trouble sleeping, at least not because of the unreal feelings or whatever. I actually usually sleep pretty soundly once I finally get to bed. \n\nThough getting to bed is a whole process, honestly. I have this mattress, it's one of those memory foam ones that comes rolled up in a box, you know? You cut the plastic and it just inflates itself, which is honestly fascinating to watch. But the thing is, they tell you it sleeps cool, and that is a complete lie. It traps heat like an oven. I've tried everything. I bought these bamboo sheets that were supposed to be breathable, but they wrinkle so badly in the wash. If you don't take them out of the dryer the exact second it stops—which, with the squeaking, I try not to run it at night anyway—they look like a crumpled-up paper bag. And ironing sheets? Forget it. Who has time to iron sheets? My grandmother used to iron everything, even her pillowcases, but she had this massive steam press machine in her basement. It looked like something out of a dry cleaner's...\n\nWait, sorry. Sleep. Yeah, no, my sleep is fine. I'm not having any trouble with that.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like these unreal feelings are affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Friends or family? I mean, not really. Like I said, I've been keeping to myself. My parents passed a while ago, and I don't really talk to my brother much. He moved out to Arizona a few years back. Scottsdale, I think. Or maybe Sedona? One of those places that's just entirely golf courses and beige stucco houses. \n\nHe's always trying to get me to come visit, especially during tax season, which is just ridiculous. I'm a tax preparer, I can't just take a week off in March. He says, \"Oh, bring your laptop, work from the patio.\" But it doesn't work like that. You need dual monitors for the spreadsheets, you need a secure connection, and honestly, the glare from the sun out there would make it impossible to see the screen anyway. Plus, I burn so easily. I'd have to buy all those SPF shirts, the ones that look like fishing gear, and I just don't think I could pull that look off. My brother wears them constantly now, along with these wraparound sunglasses that make him look like a professional cyclist. It's wild how people just adopt a whole new wardrobe when they move to a different time zone...\n\nBut no, to answer your question, I haven't noticed these feelings messing with my relationships. I'm just sort of laying low.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these unreal feelings are more related to your anxiety about the client who threatened you or to physical sensations in your body?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I mean, I guess it could be the anxiety about that guy. But physical sensations? I haven't really thought about it like that. It's just this weird feeling, you know? \n\nSpeaking of physical sensations, though, I did tweak my lower back the other day. I was trying to move this heavy filing cabinet in my home office. It's one of those old metal ones, four drawers, probably weighs a hundred pounds empty. I should have emptied it first, but I was in a rush because I was trying to make room for this new ergonomic chair I bought online. It’s supposed to be amazing for your posture, has all these adjustable lumbar supports and armrests that move in like six different directions. But the box it came in was massive, and it sat in my hallway for three days because I couldn't figure out how to put the wheels on. The instructions were completely useless—just these tiny diagrams with no words, like reading hieroglyphics. I swear, companies do that on purpose to save money on printing translations. \n\nAnyway, I finally got it together, but now my back has this dull ache right at the base of my spine. I’ve been using a heating pad, which helps a little, but it has this auto-shutoff feature after twenty minutes, which is so annoying when you just want to sit there and let the heat soak in...\n\nUm, what were we talking about? I mean, I think I just answered that, didn't I? I really don't know. It could be the anxiety about that client, or maybe it's something physical, but I haven't really been able to pin it down.

At first, I thought it was just a loose piece of plastic, maybe from when I had the cabin air filter replaced. If I push it to 50, it stops. It's the strangest thing. I took it to my mechanic, Dave, and he drove it around the block and said he couldn't hear anything, which is classic. The minute you try to show someone the problem, it magically fixes itself. 

But yeah, the unreal feelings... I don't know what's causing them. I wish I had a better answer for you, but I just don't.
